# 4.2.2 Feasibility

Here we prove item 3 of lemma 4.5, analyzing the effect of Algorithm 2 on approximate  $x$  feasibility. Our proof proceeds by first showing that if instead of taking the step we take in Algorithm 2, we instead took an idealized step where  $\overline{\mathbf{H}}$  was replaced with  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top} \overline{\mathbf{A}}$  and  $\mathbf{R}$  was replaced with  $\mathbf{I}$ , then the resulting  $x$  would have exactly met the linear feasibility constraints. We then bound the difference between linear feasibility of the actual step and this idealized step. Ultimately, the proof reveals that so long as  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top} \mathbf{R} \overline{\mathbf{A}}$  approximates  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top} \overline{\mathbf{A}}$ , and  $\overline{\mathbf{H}}$  approximates  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top} \overline{\mathbf{A}}$  well enough and the step is not too large, then the infeasibility is not too large either.

Lemma 4.12 (Feasibility Bound). In Algorithm 2 if  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\approx_{\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}$  then

$$
\left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {(\text {n e w})} - b \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X} ^ {(\text {n e w})} (\mathbf {S} ^ {(\text {n e w})}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A}} \leq 0. 5 \epsilon \gamma \left(\mu^ {(\text {n e w})}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \tag {24}
$$

and consequently, this holds with probability at least  $1 - r$ .

Proof. Recall that  $x^{\mathrm{(new)}} = x + \delta_x$  where  $\delta_x = \overline{\mathbf{X}}(g - \mathbf{R}\delta_r)$  and  $\delta_r \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \delta_p + \delta_c$ . Further, recall that

$$
\delta_ {p} = \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g \text {a n d} \delta_ {c} = \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b).
$$

Consequently, we can rewrite the step as

$$
\delta_ {r} = \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} d \text {w h e r e} d \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g + \mu^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b).
$$

Now, consider the idealized step  $x^{*}$  where there is no matrix approximation error, i.e.  $\overline{\mathbf{H}} = \overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}$ , and no sampling error, i.e.  $\mathbf{I} = \mathbf{R}$ . Formally,  $x^{*} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} x + \delta_{x}^{*}$  where  $\delta_{x}^{*} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \overline{\mathbf{X}}(g - \delta_{r}^{*})$ , and  $\delta_{r}^{*} = \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1/2}\overline{\mathbf{A}}(\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}})^{-1}d$ . Now since,  $\mathbf{A}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}} = \sqrt{\mu}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1/2}$  we have

$$
\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {*} = \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x + \sqrt {\mu} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {W} ^ {1 / 2} g - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1} d\right) = \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b) = b.
$$

Thus, we see that  $x^{*}$  obeys the linear constraints for feasibility. Consequently, it suffices to bound the error induced by matrix approximation error and sampling error, i.e.

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b = \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} (x - x ^ {*}) = - \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} (\mathbf {R} \delta_ {r} - \delta_ {r} ^ {*}) = \sqrt {\mu} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \left(\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1}\right) d \\ = \sqrt {\mu} \left(\mathbf {I} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}\right) d \tag {25} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used again that  $\mathbf{A}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}} = \sqrt{\mu}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1 / 2}$  and that diagonal matrices commute.

Now, since  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\approx_{\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\approx_{\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{H}}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {I} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2} = \| (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \\ \leq \exp (\gamma) \| \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \leq 3 \gamma \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used Lemma 4.32 and  $(2\gamma + 4\gamma^2)\exp(\gamma) \leq 3\gamma$  for  $\gamma \leq 1/80$ . Consequently, combining with (25) yields that<sup>9</sup>

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})} - b \| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1}} = \sqrt {\mu} \left\| (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {I} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} d \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq 3 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} \| d \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \leq 3 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} \left(\| \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} + \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Further, by design of  $g$  and that  $\overline{\mathbf{W}}\approx_{3\epsilon}\mathbf{I}$  and  $3\epsilon \leq \gamma$

$$
\| \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \leq \exp (\gamma / 2) \| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top}} \leq \exp (\gamma / 2) \| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {2} \leq \exp (2 \gamma) \| g \| _ {2} \leq 2 \gamma
$$

and by the approximate primal feasibility of  $(x,s,\mu)$  we have

$$
\left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \right\| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \leq \exp (\gamma / 2) \left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X S} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A}} \leq 2 \epsilon \gamma \sqrt {\mu}.
$$

Combining yields that

$$
\left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})} - b \right\| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1}} \leq 3 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} \left(2 \gamma + 2 \epsilon \gamma\right) \leq 9 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} \gamma \leq 0. 2 5 \epsilon \gamma \sqrt {\mu}.
$$

Now, since  $\| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\infty}\leq 2\gamma$  and  $\| \mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\infty}\leq 2\gamma$  by Lemma 4.8 and Lemma 4.9 and  $\gamma \leq \epsilon /100$  we have that  $\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(\mathrm{new})}(\mathbf{S}^{(\mathrm{new})})^{-1}\mathbf{A}\approx_{\epsilon}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}$ . Combining with the facts that  $\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\approx_{2\epsilon}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}$ , and  $\mu^{(\mathrm{new})}\approx_{2\epsilon \gamma}\mu$  yields (24). The probability bound follows by the fact that  $q_{i}\geq C\sigma_{i}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\log (n / (\epsilon r))\gamma^{-2}$  and Lemma 4 in [CLM  $^+$  15]. That is, the matrix  $\mathbf{R}$  corresponds to sampling each row of  $\mathbf{A}$  with probability at least proportional to its leverage score.

# 4.2.3 Proof of Lemma 4.5

We conclude by proving Lemma 4.5, the main claim of this section.

Proof of Lemma 4.5. (1) Note that  $r = \frac{\epsilon\gamma}{\sqrt{m}} = \frac{\epsilon^2}{100\lambda\sqrt{m}} = \frac{\epsilon^3}{3600\log(240\beta)\sqrt{m}}$  for  $\beta = \frac{m}{\epsilon^2}$ . Thus,

$$
1 2 \epsilon^ {- 1} \log (1 6 m / r) \leq 1 2 \epsilon^ {- 1} \log (1 6 \beta^ {3 / 2} \cdot 3 6 0 0 \log (2 4 0 \beta)) \leq 1 2 \epsilon^ {- 1} \log ((2 4 0) ^ {3} \beta^ {2}) \leq \lambda
$$

where we used that  $\sqrt{x}\log x\leq x$  for  $x\geq 1$ $(240)^{3} = 16\cdot 3600\cdot 240$  , and  $\beta \geq 1$

(2) Note that  $\delta_s = \mu^{1/2} \mathbf{A} \overline{\mathbf{H}}^{-1} \overline{\mathbf{A}}^\top \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1/2} g$ . Consequently,  $\delta_s \in \mathrm{im}(\mathbf{A})$  and this follows from the fact that  $\mathbf{A} y + s = c$  for some  $y \in \mathbb{R}^n$  by definition of  $\epsilon$ -centered.  
(3) and (4) these follow immediately from Lemma 4.11 and Lemma 4.12 respectively.

# 4.3 Short-step Method for Lee-Sidford Weights

In this section we show how to efficiently implement a short step when the weight function is regularized variant of the weights induced by Lee-Sidford barriers [LS19], i.e. it is defined as follows and for brevity, we refer to it as the Lee-Sidford (LS)-weight function.

Definition 4.13 (Lee-Sidford Weight Function). We define the Lee-Sidford (LS) weight function for all  $x, s \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m}$  as  $\tau(x, s) \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \sigma(x, s) + \frac{n}{m} \vec{1}$  where  $\sigma(x, s) \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \sigma(\mathbf{X}^{1/2 - \alpha} \mathbf{S}^{-1/2 - \alpha} \mathbf{A})$  for  $\alpha \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \frac{1}{4 \log(4m/n)}$ , and  $\sigma$  defined as in Section 2.

This weight function is analogous to the one used in [BLSS20] which in turn is inspired by the weight functions and barriers used in [LS19] (see these works for further intuition).

Our short-step algorithm for this barrier (Algorithm 2) is very similar to the one for the logarithmic barrier (Algorithm 3). The primary difference is what  $g$  is set to in the two algorithms. In both algorithms  $g$  is chosen to approximate the negation of the gradient of the centrality potential while being small in requisite norms for the method. However, by choosing a different weight function both the gradient and the norm change. To explain the new step (and analyze our method) throughout this section we make extensive use of the following mixed norm and its dual vector (variants of which were defined in [LS19, BLSS20]).

Definition 4.14 (Mixed Norm and Dual Vector). We define the mixed norm  $\| x\|_{\tau +\infty}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\| x\|_{\infty}+$ $C_\mathrm{norm}\| x\|_{\tau}$  with  $C_\mathrm{norm} = \frac{10}{\alpha}$  and we define the dual vector of  $g$  as

$$
g ^ {\flat (w)} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \operatorname * {a r g m a x} _ {\| x \| _ {w + \infty} = 1} g ^ {\top} x. \tag {26}
$$

Further we define the dual norm  $\| g\|_{\tau +\infty}^{*}\coloneqq \langle g,g^{\flat (\tau)}\rangle$  , i.e. the maximum attained by (26).

With this notation in mind, recall that in the short-step algorithm for the logarithmic barrier (Algorithm 3)) we chose  $g$  to approximate the direction which has best inner product with  $-\nabla \Phi(w)$  and has  $\|\cdot\|_2$  at most  $\gamma$ . Formally, we picked  $\overline{v}$  with  $\|\overline{v} - w\|_{\gamma}$  and let  $g = -\gamma \nabla (\Phi(\overline{v})) / \|\nabla (\Phi(\overline{v}))\|_2$ . Note that  $g = -\gamma g^{\flat(\overline{v})}$  if in (26) we replaced  $\|x\|_{w + \infty}$  with  $\|x\|_2$ . Since when using the Lee-Sidford weight function (Definition 4.13) we instead wish to decrease  $\Phi(w / \tau(x, s))$  we instead pick  $\overline{v} \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^m$  such that  $\|\overline{v} - w / \tau(x, s)\|_{\infty} \leq \gamma$  and let  $g \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} -\gamma \nabla \Phi(\overline{v})^{\flat(\overline{\tau})}$  where  $\flat$  is as defined in Definition 4.14.

This setting of  $g$  is the primary difference between the algorithm in this section (Algorithm 3) and the one in previous section (Algorithm 2). The other difference are induced by this change or done for technical or analysis reasons. We change the setting of  $r$  and the requirements for  $q$  to account for the fewer iterations we take and the different norm in which we analyze our error. Further, we slightly change the step sizes for  $\delta_x$  and  $\delta_s$ , i.e. in Algorithm 3 we let

$$
\delta_ {x} = \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} ((1 + 2 \alpha) g - \mathbf {R} \delta_ {r}) \mathrm {a n d} \delta_ {s} = (1 - 2 \alpha) \overline {{\mathbf {S}}} \delta_ {p}
$$

(whereas in Algorithm 2 we set  $\delta_x$  and  $\delta_s$  as above with  $\alpha = 0$ ). This change was also performed in [BLSS20] to account for the effect of  $\tau$  on the centrality potential and ensure sufficient progress is made.

Algorithm 3: Short Step (LS Weight Function)  
1 procedure SHORTSTEP  $(x\in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m},s\in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m},\mu \in \mathbb{R}_{>0},\mu^{(\mathrm{new})}\in \mathbb{R}_{>0})$    
\*/ Fix  $\tau (x,s)\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\sigma (x,s) + \frac{n}{m}\vec{1}\in \mathbb{R}^m$  for all  $x\in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m},s\in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m}$    
\*/ Let  $\lambda = \frac{36\log(400m / \epsilon^2)}{\epsilon}$ $\gamma = \frac{\epsilon}{100\lambda}$ $r = \frac{\epsilon\gamma}{\sqrt{n}}$ $r = \frac{\epsilon\gamma}{240}$ $\alpha = \frac{1}{4\log(\frac{4m}{n})}$    
2 Assume  $(x,s,\mu)$  is  $\epsilon$  -centered   
3 Assume  $\delta_{\mu}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\mu^{(\mathrm{new})} - \mu$  satisfies  $|\delta_{\mu}|\leq r\mu$    
Approximate the iterate and compute step direction   
4 Find  $\overline{x},\overline{s},\overline{\tau}$  such that  $\overline{x}\approx_{\epsilon}x$ $s,\overline{s}\approx_{\epsilon}s$ $\overline{\tau}\approx_{\epsilon}\tau$  for  $\tau \stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=} \tau (x,s)$    
5 Find  $\overline{v}\in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m}$  such that  $\| \overline{v} -w / \tau \|_{\infty}\leq \gamma$  for  $w\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}w(x,s,\mu)$    
6  $g = -\gamma \nabla \Phi (\overline{v})^{b(\overline{\tau})}$  where  $h^{\flat (w)}$  is defined in Definition 4.14   
/\* Sample rows   
7  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{1 / 2}\overline{\mathbf{S}}^{-1 / 2}\mathbf{A},\overline{w}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}w(\overline{x},\overline{s},\mu),\overline{\mathbf{X}}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}Diag(\overline{x}),\overline{\mathbf{S}}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}Diag(\overline{s}),\overline{\mathbf{W}}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}Diag(\overline{w})$    
8 Find  $\overline{\mathbf{H}}$  such that  $\overline{\mathbf{H}}\approx_{\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}$    
9 Let  $\delta_p\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1 / 2}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\overline{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^\top \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1 / 2}g$ $\delta_c\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}~\mu^{-1 / 2}\cdot \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1 / 2}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\overline{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}(\mathbf{A}^\top x - b)$  ,and   
 $\delta_r\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}(1 + 2\alpha)\delta_p + \delta_c$    
10 Find  $q\in \mathbb{R}_>^n$  such that  $q_{i}\geq \frac{m}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot ((\delta_{r})_{i}^{2} / \| \delta_{r}\|_{2}^{2} + 1 / m) + C\cdot \sigma_{i}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\log (m / (\epsilon r))\gamma^{-2}$    
for some large enough constant  $C$    
11 Sample a diagonal matrix  $\mathbf{R}\in \mathbb{R}^{m\times m}$  where each  ${\bf R}_{i,i} = [\min \{q_i,1\}]^{-1}$  independently with probability min  $\left\{q_i,1\right\}$  and  ${\bf R}_{i,i} = 0$  otherwise   
/\* Compute projected step   
12  $\delta_x\gets \overline{\mathbf{x}} ((1 + 2\alpha)g - {\bf R}\delta_r)$    
13  $\delta_s\gets (1 - 2\alpha)\overline{\mathbf{S}}\delta_p$    
14  $x^{(\mathrm{new})}\gets x + \delta_x$  and  $s^{(\mathrm{new})}\gets s + \delta_s$    
15 return  $(x^{(\mathrm{new})},s^{(\mathrm{new})})$

We analyze the performance of this short-step procedure for the Lee-Sidford barrier analogously to how we analyzed the short step procedure for the log barrier in Section 4.2. We again apply Lemma 4.34 and Lemma 4.36 and together these lemmas reduce bounding the potential  $\Phi(x^{(\mathrm{new})}, s^{(\mathrm{new})}, \mu^{(\mathrm{new})})$  to carefully analyzing bounding the first moments, second moments, and multiplicative stability induced by  $\delta_s$ ,  $\delta_x$ ,  $\delta_\mu$  as well as the change from  $\tau(x, s)$  to  $\tau(x^{(\mathrm{new})}, s^{(\mathrm{new})})$ , which we denote by  $\delta_\tau \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \tau(x^{(\mathrm{new})}, s^{(\mathrm{new})}) - \tau(x, s)$ .

Key differences in the analysis of this section in the previous section are in how these bounds are achieved and in analyzing  $\delta_{\tau}$ . Whereas the analysis of the log barrier short-step method primarily considered the  $\ell_2$  norm, here we consider the mixed norm, Definition 4.14 (variants of which were analyzed in [LS19, BLSS20]). For  $\delta_x$  and  $\delta_s$  this analysis is performed in Section 4.3.1. For analyzing  $\delta_{\tau}$  new analysis, though related to calculations in [BLSS20], is provided in Section 4.3.2. This analysis is then leveraged in Section 4.3.3 to provide the main result of this section, that Algorithm 3 is a valid short-step procedure.

# 4.3.1 Bounding  $\delta_{x}$  and  $\delta_{s}$

Here we provide basic bounds on  $\delta_x$ ,  $\delta_s$ , and related quantities. First, we provide Lemma 4.15 and Lemma 4.16 which are restatements of lemmas from [BLSS20] which immediately apply to our algorithm. Leveraging these lemmas, we state and prove Lemma 4.17 which bounds the effect of our feasibility correction step (which was not present in the same way [BLSS20]). Leveraging these lemmas we prove Lemma 4.18, which provides first-order bounds and then we prove Lemma 4.19 which provides second-order and multiplicative bounds.

Lemma 4.15 ([BLSS20, Lemma 18]). In Algorithm 3, we have

$$
\frac {1}{2} \sigma (\overline {{\mathbf {S}}} ^ {- 1 / 2 - \alpha} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} ^ {1 / 2 - \alpha} \mathbf {A}) \leq \sigma (\overline {{\mathbf {S}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {A}) \leq 2 \sigma (\overline {{\mathbf {S}}} ^ {- 1 / 2 - \alpha} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} ^ {1 / 2 - \alpha} \mathbf {A}).
$$

Lemma 4.16 ([BLSS20, Lemma 19]). In Algorithm 3,  $\mathbf{Q} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \overline{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}$  satisfies  $\mathbf{Q} \preceq e^{3\epsilon}\mathbf{P}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})$ . Further, for all  $h \in \mathbb{R}^m$ ,

$$
\| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} h \| _ {\tau} \leq e ^ {4 \epsilon} \| h \| _ {\tau} a n d \| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {I} - \mathbf {Q}) \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} h \| _ {\tau} \leq e ^ {3 \epsilon} \| h \| _ {\tau}.
$$

Further,  $\| \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1 / 2}\mathbf{Q}\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1 / 2}h\|_{\infty}\leq 2\| h\|_{\tau}$  and therefore  $\| \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1 / 2}\mathbf{Q}\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1 / 2}\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq e^{4\epsilon} + 2 / C_{\mathrm{norm}}$

Lemma 4.17. In Algorithm 3,

$$
\| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} g \| _ {\tau} \leq e ^ {2 \epsilon} \| g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} a n d \| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} g \| _ {\infty} \leq 2 \| g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}}.
$$

Proof. Let  $\mathbf{Q} = \overline{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}$ . Since  $\overline{\mathbf{H}}\succeq e^{-\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\succeq e^{-\epsilon}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}$ , we have

$$
\| \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = \| \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2} \leq e ^ {\epsilon} \| \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2} \leq e ^ {\epsilon}.
$$

Since  $\gamma \leq \epsilon$  we have  $\overline{w} \approx_{2\epsilon} w$  and  $w \approx_{\epsilon} \tau$ . This implies  $\overline{w} \approx_{3\epsilon} \tau$  and

$$
\| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} g \| _ {\tau} \leq e ^ {1. 5 \epsilon} \| \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} g \| _ {2} \leq e ^ {1. 5 \epsilon} \| \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \| \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {2} \leq e ^ {2 \epsilon} \| g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}}.
$$

Further, by Cauchy Schwarz, we have

$$
\| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} g \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} = \max _ {i \in [ m ]} \left[ e _ {i} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} g \right] ^ {2} \leq \max _ {i \in [ m ]} \left[ \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right] _ {i, i} \cdot \left[ g ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1} g \right]
$$

$\mathbf{Q} \preceq e^{3\epsilon}\mathbf{P}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})$  (Lemma 4.16) and  $\sigma (\overline{\mathbf{A}})_i \leq 2\sigma (\overline{\mathbf{S}}^{-1 / 2 - \alpha}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{1 / 2 - \alpha}\mathbf{A})_i \leq 3\tau_i$  (Lemma 4.15), implies

$$
\max _ {i \in [ m ]} \left[ \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right] _ {i, i} \leq \max _ {i \in [ m ]} \frac {e ^ {3 \epsilon} \sigma (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) _ {i}}{\overline {{w}} _ {i}} \leq e ^ {6 \epsilon} \max _ {i \in [ m ]} \frac {\sigma (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) _ {i}}{\tau_ {i}} \leq 3 e ^ {6 \epsilon}.
$$

Lemma 4.18 (Basic Step Size Bounds). In Algorithm 3, we have that

$$
\left\| \delta_ {p} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {1 0}{9} \gamma \text {a n d} \left\| \delta_ {c} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {1 1 \epsilon \gamma}{\alpha} \leq \frac {2}{7} \gamma \tag {27}
$$

and consequently

$$
\| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ \| \delta_ {x} | ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 2 \gamma a n d \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 2 \gamma .
$$

Proof. We first bound  $\delta_p$ . Let  $\mathbf{Q} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \overline{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^\top$  and note that

$$
\delta_ {p} = \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g. \tag {28}
$$

By Lemma 4.16, we have  $\| \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1 / 2}\mathbf{Q}\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1 / 2}g\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq (e^{4\epsilon} + 2 / C_{\mathrm{norm}})\| g\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq \frac{10}{9}\| g\|_{\tau +\infty}$ .

Now, we bound  $\delta_c$ . Recall that

$$
\delta_ {c} = \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b).
$$

By Lemma 4.17, we have

$$
\| \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b) \| _ {\tau} \leq \frac {e ^ {2 \epsilon}}{\sqrt {\mu}} \| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \leq \frac {e ^ {3 \epsilon}}{\sqrt {\mu}} \| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \| _ {(\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X S} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A}) ^ {- 1}}.
$$

Hence, we have  $\| \delta_c\|_{\tau}\leq e^{3\epsilon}\gamma \epsilon$  . For the sup norm, Lemma 4.17, shows that

$$
\| \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b) \| _ {\infty} \leq \frac {2}{\sqrt {\mu}} \| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \leq \frac {2 e ^ {\epsilon}}{\sqrt {\mu}} \| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \| _ {(\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X S} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A}) ^ {- 1}}.
$$

Hence, we have  $\| \delta_c\|_{\infty}\leq 2e^{\epsilon}\gamma \epsilon$  by definition of  $\epsilon$ -centered. In particular, as  $\epsilon \leq \alpha /80$ , we have

$$
\left\| \delta_ {c} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 2 e ^ {\epsilon} \gamma \epsilon + C _ {\mathrm {n o r m}} e ^ {3 \epsilon} \gamma \epsilon = \left(2 e ^ {\epsilon} + \frac {1 0 e ^ {3 \epsilon}}{\alpha}\right) \gamma \epsilon \leq \frac {1 1 \gamma \epsilon}{\alpha} \leq \frac {2}{7} \gamma .
$$

For the bound of  $\mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s$ , we note that  $\delta_{s} = (1 - 2\alpha)\overline{\mathbf{S}}\delta_{p}$  and hence

$$
\| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {S}}} \delta_ {p} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 1. 1 \cdot \| \delta_ {p} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 2 \gamma .
$$

For the bound of  $\mathbf{X}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[|\delta_x|]$ , note that  $\mathbf{X}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[|\delta_x|] = \mathbf{X}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{X}}|(1 + 2\alpha)(g - \delta_p) - \delta_c|$  and (28) yields

$$
g - \delta_ {p} = \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {I} - \mathbf {Q}) \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g.
$$

By Lemma 4.16, we have  $\| g - \delta_p\|_{\tau}\leq e^{3\epsilon}\| g\|_{\tau}$  and  $\| g - \delta_p\|_{\infty}\leq \| g\|_{\infty} + \| \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1 / 2}\mathbf{Q}\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1 / 2}g\|_{\infty}\leq$ $\| g\|_{\infty} + 2\| g\|_{\tau}$ . Hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| g - \delta_ {p} \| _ {\tau + \infty} = \| g - \delta_ {p} \| _ {\infty} + C _ {\mathrm {n o r m}} \| g - \delta_ {p} \| _ {\tau} \\ \leq \| g \| _ {\infty} + 2 \| g \| _ {\tau} + C _ {\mathrm {n o r m}} e ^ {3 \epsilon} \| g \| _ {\tau} \\ \leq \left(e ^ {3 \epsilon} + \frac {2}{C _ {\mathrm {n o r m}}}\right) \| g \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 1. 1 \gamma . \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ | \delta_ {x} | ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 1. 1 \| (1 + 2 \alpha) (g - \delta_ {p}) - \delta_ {c} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq (1. 1) ^ {2} (1 + 2 \alpha) \gamma + \frac {2}{7} \gamma \\ \leq (1. 1) ^ {2} \cdot (1 + 2 / 5) \gamma + \frac {2}{7} \gamma \leq 2 \gamma . \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third step follows  $\alpha \in [0,1 / 5]$

Lemma 4.19 (Multiplicative Stability and Second-order Analysis of  $x$  and  $s$ ). In Algorithm 3,

-  $\| \mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x\|_\infty \leq 2\gamma$  and  $\| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_\infty \leq 2\gamma$  with probability 1.  
$\| \mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x)^2 ]\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 12\gamma^2$  and  $\| (\mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s)^2\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 4\gamma^2$

In particular, we have  $\mathbb{E}\left[\| \mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\phi^{\prime \prime}(v)}^2\right]\leq 12\gamma^2\| \phi ''(v)\|_{\tau +\infty}^*$ . and  $\| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\phi ''(v)}^2\leq 4\gamma^2\| \phi ''(v)\|_{\tau +\infty}^*$ .

Proof. For sup norm for  $s$ , it follows from  $\| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\infty}\leq \| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\tau +\infty}$  and Lemma 4.18.

For sup norm for  $x$ , recall that

$$
\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} = \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} \big ((1 + 2 \alpha) g - \mathbf {R} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \big).
$$

There are two cases.

Case 1)  $i$ -th row is sampled with probability 1. We have

$$
\left(\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}\right) _ {i} = \mathbb {E} [ \left(\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}\right) _ {i} ]
$$

By Lemma 4.18, we have  $|(\mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_{x})_{i}| \leq 2\gamma$ .

Case 2)  $i$ -th row is sampled with probability at least  $\frac{m}{\sqrt{n}} \cdot \left( (\delta_r)_i^2 / \| \delta_r \|_2^2 + \frac{1}{m} \right) \leq 1$  and this quantity is at most 1. In this case, (22) of the proof too the analogous Lemma 4.9, still applies to yield that  $|[ \delta_r ]_i | \leq (\sqrt{m} / 2) \| \delta_r \|_2 \cdot ([\delta_r]_i^2 / \| \delta_r \|_2^2 + 1/m)$  and therefore

$$
\begin{array}{l} | (\mathbf {R} \delta_ {r}) _ {i} | \leq \frac {| (\delta_ {r}) _ {i} |}{q _ {i}} \leq \frac {\sqrt {n}}{m} \frac {| (\delta_ {r}) _ {i} |}{(\delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2} / \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{m}} \leq \frac {\sqrt {n}}{m} \cdot \frac {\sqrt {m}}{2} \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {2} \\ \leq \frac {\sqrt {n}}{m} \cdot \frac {\sqrt {m}}{2} \cdot \sqrt {\frac {m}{n}} \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau} = \frac {1}{2} \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\tau \geq \frac{n}{m}$  in the last inequality. Using  $\overline{x} \approx_{\epsilon} x$ , we have

$$
\left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \right\| _ {\infty} \leq \frac {3}{2} \| g \| _ {\infty} + \frac {2}{3} \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau}. \tag {29}
$$

Finally, by  $\delta_r = (1 + 2\alpha)\delta_p + \delta_c$  and Lemma 4.18, we have

$$
\left\| \delta_ {r} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 1. 4 \left\| \delta_ {p} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} + \left\| \delta_ {c} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {1 3}{7} \gamma . \tag {30}
$$

Now, using  $C_{\mathrm{norm}} \geq 50$ , we have

$$
\left\| \delta_ {r} \right\| _ {\tau} \leq \frac {1}{C _ {\text {n o r m}}} \left\| \delta_ {r} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {1}{5 0} \left\| \delta_ {r} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {\gamma}{4 0}. \tag {31}
$$

This gives the bound  $\| \mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\infty}\leq 2\gamma$

For the second order term for  $s$ , leveraging the generalization of Cauchy Schwarz to arbitrary norms, i.e. that for every norm  $\| \cdot \|$ , its dual  $\| \cdot \|^{*}$ , and all  $x, y$  we have  $|x^{\top}y| \leq \| x\| \| y\|^{*}$ , yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \right\| _ {\phi^ {\prime \prime} (v)} ^ {2} = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v _ {i}) (\mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s}) _ {i} ^ {2} \leq \| (\mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s}) ^ {2} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*} \\ \leq \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \| _ {\infty} \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*} \leq 4 \gamma^ {2} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For the second order term for  $x$ , we note that

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\phi^ {\prime \prime} (v)} ^ {2} \right] = \sum_ {i \in [ m ]} \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v _ {i}) \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}) _ {i} ^ {2} ] \leq \| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*}.
$$

Hence, it suffices to bound  $\| \mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x)^2 ]\|_{\tau +\infty}$ . Using that  $\delta_{x} = \overline{\mathbf{X}} ((1 + 2\alpha)g - \mathbf{R}\delta_{r})$ , we have

$$
(\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}) _ {i} ^ {2} \leq 2 ((1 + 2 \alpha) \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} g) _ {i} ^ {2} + 2 (\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} \mathbf {R} \delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2}
$$

Using  $\overline{x} \approx_{\epsilon} x$  and  $\alpha \leq \frac{1}{5}$ , we have

$$
\left(\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}\right) _ {i} ^ {2} \leq 4 g _ {i} ^ {2} + 2. 1 \left(\mathbf {R} \delta_ {r}\right) _ {i} ^ {2}. \tag {32}
$$

For the second term, we note that  $i$ -th row is sampled with probability at least  $\min(1, \frac{m}{\sqrt{n}} \cdot (\delta_r)_i^2 / \| \delta_r \|_2^2)$ . Hence, we have

$$
\mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {R} \delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2} ] \leq (\delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2} + \frac {(\delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2}}{\frac {m}{\sqrt {n}} \cdot (\delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2} / \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {2} ^ {2}} \leq (\delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2} + \frac {\sqrt {n}}{m} \cdot \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq (\delta_ {r}) _ {i} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau} ^ {2}.
$$

Hence, we have

$$
\| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {R} \delta_ {r}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \| (\delta_ {r}) ^ {2} \| _ {\tau + \infty} + \frac {1}{\sqrt {n}} \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau} ^ {2} \cdot \| 1 \| _ {\tau + \infty}.
$$

Since  $\| 1\|_{\tau +\infty} = \| 1\|_{\infty} + C_{\mathrm{norm}}\| 1\|_{\tau} = 1 + \sqrt{2n} C_{\mathrm{norm}}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {R} \delta_ {r}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {2} + \frac {5}{2} C _ {\text {n o r m}} \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau} ^ {2} \leq \left(1 + \frac {5}{2 C _ {\text {n o r m}}}\right) \| \delta_ {r} \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left(1 + \frac {5}{1 0 0}\right) \cdot \left(\frac {1 3}{7}\right) ^ {2} \gamma^ {2} \leq 3. 7 \gamma^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used (30) at the third inequalities.

Putting this into (32) gives

$$
\| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 4 \| g ^ {2} \| _ {\tau + \infty} + (2. 1) (3. 7) \gamma^ {2} \leq 4 \| g \| _ {\tau + \infty} \| g \| _ {\infty} + (2. 1) (3. 7) \gamma^ {2} \leq 1 2 \gamma^ {2}.
$$

# 4.3.2 Bounding  $\delta_{\tau}$

Here we bound the first two moments of the change in the LS weight function. First we leverage Lemma 4.20, leverage score related derivative calculations from [LS19], to provide general bounds on how  $\tau$  changes in Lemma 4.21. Then, applying a Lemma 4.22, a technical lemma about how the quadratic form of entrywise-squared projection matrices behave under sampling from [BLSS20], and Lemma 4.23, a new lemma regarding the stability  $\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{WA})^{(2)}$  under changes to  $\mathbf{W}$ , in Lemma 4.24 we bound  $\delta_{\tau} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \tau(x^{(\mathrm{new})}, s^{(\mathrm{new})}) - \tau(x, s)$  in Algorithm 3.

Lemma 4.20 ([LS19, Lemma 49]). Given a full rank  $\mathbf{A} \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times n}$  and  $w \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m}$ , we have

$$
D _ {w} \mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W A}) [ h ] = \Delta \mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W A}) + \mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W A}) \Delta - 2 \mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W A}) \Delta \mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W A})
$$

where  $\mathbf{W} = \mathbf{Diag}(w)$ ,  $\Delta = \mathbf{Diag}(h / w)$ , and  $D_{w}f[h] \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \lim t \to 0 \frac{1}{t} [f(w + th) - f(w)]$  denotes the directional derivative of  $f$  with respect to  $w$  in direction  $h$ . In particular, we have  $D_{w}\sigma(\mathbf{WA})[h] = 2\Lambda(\mathbf{WA})\mathbf{W}^{-1}h$ .

Lemma 4.21. Let  $w \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m}$  be arbitrary and let  $w^{(\mathrm{new})}$  be a random vector such that  $w^{(\mathrm{new})} \approx_{\gamma} w$  with probability 1 for some  $0 \leq \gamma \leq \frac{1}{80}$ . Let  $\tau = \sigma(\mathbf{W}\mathbf{A}) + \eta$  and  $\tau^{(\mathrm{new})} = \sigma(\mathbf{W}^{(\mathrm{new})}\mathbf{A}) + \eta$  for some  $\eta \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{m}$ . Let  $\delta_w = w^{(\mathrm{new})} - w$ ,  $\delta_\tau = \tau^{(\mathrm{new})} - \tau$  and  $\mathbf{W}_t = (1 - t)\mathbf{W} + t\mathbf{W}^{(\mathrm{new})}$ . Then, for

$$
K \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} 1 0 \| \mathbb {E} _ {w ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}} [ (\mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {w}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma (\mathbf {W A}) + \infty} + 1 3 \gamma \max _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \mathbb {E} _ {w ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}} [ \| \mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {w} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\mathbf {W} _ {t} \mathbf {A}) + \infty} ]
$$

where  $\| x\|_{M + \infty} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \| x\|_{\infty} + C\| x\|_{M}$  for some fixed constant  $C \geq 1$  and any norm  $\| \cdot \|_M$ , the following hold

$\| \mathbf{T}^{-1}\delta_{\tau}\|_{\infty}\leq 5\gamma ,$  
$\| \mathbb{E}_{w^{(\mathrm{new})}}[(\mathbf{T}^{-1}\delta_{\tau})^2 ]\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq K$

$$
\cdot \| \mathbb {E} _ {w ^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}} \left[ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \left(\tau^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)} - \tau - 2 \Lambda (\mathbf {W A}) \mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {w}\right) \right] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq K.
$$

Proof. Let  $w_{t} = w + t\delta_{w}$ ,  $\sigma_{t} = \sigma(\mathbf{W}_{t}\mathbf{A})$ ,  $\tau_{t} = \sigma(\mathbf{W}_{t}\mathbf{A}) + \eta$ . For simplicity, we let  $\sigma \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \sigma_{0}$  and  $r_{t} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{W}_{t}^{-1}\delta_{w}$ .

For the first result, recall that  $\sigma_t = \mathbf{Diag}(\mathbf{W}_t\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^\top \mathbf{W}_t^2\mathbf{A})^{-1}\mathbf{A}^\top \mathbf{W}_t)$ . Since,  $w_t \approx_{\gamma} w$ , we have that  $\sigma_t \approx_{4\gamma} \sigma$  and hence  $\| \mathbf{T}^{-1}\delta_{\tau}\|_{\infty} \leq 5\gamma$ .

For the second result, Jensen's inequality shows that

$$
\left(\mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\tau}\right) _ {i} ^ {2} = \left(\mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left[ \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \sigma_ {t} \right] \mathrm {d} t\right) _ {i} ^ {2} \leq \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left(\mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \left[ \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \sigma_ {t} \right]\right) _ {i} ^ {2} d t \tag {33}
$$

Using that  $\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sigma_t = 2\pmb{\Lambda}_t r_t$  (Lemma 4.20) and  $\pmb{\Lambda}_t = \pmb{\Sigma}_t - \mathbf{P}_t^{(2)}$ , we have

$$
\tau_ {i} ^ {- 2} \left(\left[ \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \sigma_ {t} \right] _ {i}\right) ^ {2} = 4 \tau_ {i} ^ {- 2} \left(\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} r _ {t}\right) _ {i} ^ {2} \leq 8 \tau_ {i} ^ {- 2} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} r _ {t}\right) _ {i} ^ {2} + \left(\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t}\right) _ {i} ^ {2}\right). \tag {34}
$$

Substituting (34) in (33), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbb {E} \left(\mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\tau}\right) ^ {2} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 8 \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbf {T} ^ {- 2} \mathbb {E} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} r _ {t}\right) ^ {2} + \left(\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} d t \\ \leq 8 \max _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \left(\left\| \mathbf {T} ^ {- 2} \mathbb {E} (\pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} r _ {t}) ^ {2} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} + \left\| \mathbf {T} ^ {- 2} \mathbb {E} (\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t}) \right\| _ {\tau + \infty}\right) \\ \leq 1 0 \max  _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \left(\left\| \mathbb {E} r _ {t} ^ {2} \right\| _ {\sigma + \infty} + \left\| \mathbb {E} \left(\boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t}\right) ^ {2} \right\| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty}\right) \tag {35} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $w_{t}\approx_{\gamma}w$ $\sigma_t\approx_{4\gamma}\sigma$  and  $\tau \geq \sigma$  at the end.

For the second term in (35), using  $\| \pmb{\Sigma}_t^{-1}\mathbf{P}_t^{(2)}r_t\|_\infty \leq \| r_t\|_\infty \leq \frac{9}{8}\gamma$  (Lemma 4.25),  $\pmb{\Sigma}_t\succeq \mathbf{P}_t^{(2)}$  (Lemma 4.25) and that  $\mathbf{P}_t^{(2)}$  is entry-wise non-negative, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbb {E} (\pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t}) ^ {2} \right\| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} \leq \frac {9}{8} \gamma \mathbb {E} \left\| \pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} | r _ {t} | \right\| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} \\ \leq \frac {9}{8} \gamma \mathbb {E} \| | r _ {t} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} \leq 1. 3 \gamma \mathbb {E} \| \mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {w} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} \tag {36} \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting this into (35) gives the second result.

For the third result, Taylor expansion shows that

$$
\sigma_ {1} = \sigma_ {0} + \left. \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \sigma_ {t} \right| _ {t = 0} + \int_ {0} ^ {1} (1 - t) \left[ \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {2}}{\mathrm {d} t ^ {2}} \sigma_ {t} \right] \mathrm {d} t.
$$

Using again that  $\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sigma_t = 2\pmb{\Lambda}_t r_t$  (Lemma 4.20) we have that  $\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sigma_t\right|_{t=0} = 2\pmb{\Lambda}\mathbf{W}^{-1}\delta_w$  and therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} (*) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \| \mathbb {E} _ {w ^ {(\text {n e w})}} [ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} (\tau_ {1} - \tau_ {0} - 2 \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {w}) ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \\ \leq \left\| \int_ {0} ^ {1} (1 - t) \mathbb {E} _ {w (\text {n e w})} \left[ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {2}}{\mathrm {d} t ^ {2}} \sigma_ {t} (t) \right] \mathrm {d} t \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \\ \leq \frac {1}{2} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbb {E} _ {w ^ {\text {(n e w)}}} \left[ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {2}}{\mathrm {d} t ^ {2}} \sigma_ {t} (t) \right] \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \mathrm {d} t. \tag {37} \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, it suffices to bound  $\frac{d^2}{dt^2}\sigma_t(t)$ .

Note that

$$
\frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {2}}{\mathrm {d} t ^ {2}} \sigma_ {t} (t) = 2 \left[ \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} \right] r _ {t} - 2 \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} r _ {t} ^ {2}. \tag {38}
$$

Let  $\Delta_t \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{Diag}(r_t)$ . Recall that  $\boldsymbol{\Lambda}_t = \boldsymbol{\Sigma}_t - (\mathbf{W}_t\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^\top \mathbf{W}_t^2\mathbf{A})^{-1}\mathbf{A}^\top \mathbf{W}_t)^{(2)}$ , Lemma 4.20 shows

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} = \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} - 2 \mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ \frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t} (\mathbf {W} _ {t} \mathbf {A} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {W} _ {t} ^ {2} \mathbf {A}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {W} _ {t}) \\ = 2 \mathbf {D i a g} (\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} r _ {t}) - 2 \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} - 2 \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \Delta_ {t} + 4 \mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Using this in (38) gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\mathrm {d} ^ {2}}{\mathrm {d} t ^ {2}} \sigma_ {t} (t) = 4 \mathbf {D i a g} (\boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} r _ {t}) \cdot \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} - 4 \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} - 4 \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \Delta_ {t} r _ {t} + 8 (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t})) r _ {t} - 2 \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} r _ {t} ^ {2} \\ = 4 \Delta_ {t} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} _ {t} r _ {t} - 4 \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} - 4 \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ^ {2} + 8 (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t})) r _ {t} - 2 \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} r _ {t} ^ {2} + 2 \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ^ {2} \\ = 2 \pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} r _ {t} ^ {2} - 8 \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} - 2 \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ^ {2} + 8 (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t})) r _ {t}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting this into (37) and using  $w_{t}\approx_{\gamma}w$ $\sigma_t\approx_{4\gamma}\sigma$  , and  $\tau \geq \sigma$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} (*) \leq \max _ {t} \left[ \| \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} r _ {t} ^ {2} \| _ {\tau + \infty} ] + 4 \| \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \right] \\ + \max _ {t} \left[ \| \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ^ {2} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} + 4 \| \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t})) r _ {t} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \right] \\ \leq \max _ {t} \left[ 1. 2 \| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {w}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma + \infty} + 5 \| r _ {t} \| _ {\infty} \| \mathbb {E} [ \pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ] \| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} \right] \\ + \max _ {t} \left[ 1. 2 \| \mathbb {E} [ \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} + 5 \| \mathbb {E} [ \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t})) r _ {t} ] \| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} \right]. \tag {39} \\ \end{array}
$$

For the second term in (39), we follow a similar argument as (36) and get

$$
\| \mathbb {E} [ \pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ] \| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} \leq \mathbb {E} [ \| \pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} \| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} ] \leq \mathbb {E} \Big [ \| | r _ {t} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} \pmb {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} \Big ] \leq \mathbb {E} \Big [ \| | r _ {t} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} \Big ].
$$

Similarly, the third term in (39), we have

$$
\| \mathbb {E} [ \boldsymbol {\Sigma} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma_ {t} + \infty} \leq \mathbb {E} \Big [ \| r _ {t} ^ {2} \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} \Big ].
$$

For the fourth term, note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {i} ^ {\top} (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \circ (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t})) r _ {t} = \sum_ {j \in [ m ]} (\mathbf {P} _ {t}) _ {i, j} (\mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t}) _ {i, j} [ r _ {t} ] _ {j} = e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} \mathbf {P} _ {t} e _ {i} \\ \leq e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {P} _ {t} \Delta_ {t} ^ {2} \mathbf {P} _ {t} e _ {i} = e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} r _ {t} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, we can bound the term again by  $\mathbb{E}\| r_t^2\|_{\mathbf{P}_t^{(2)}}^2$ . In summary, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} (*) \leq 1. 2 \| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {w}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma + \infty} + 5 \| r _ {t} \| _ {\infty} \mathbb {E} [ \| | r _ {t} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} ] + 6. 2 \mathbb {E} [ \| r _ {t} ^ {2} \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} ] \\ \leq 1. 2 \| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {w}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma + \infty} + 1 1. 2 \| r _ {t} \| _ {\infty} \mathbb {E} [ \| | r _ {t} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} ] \\ \leq 1. 2 \| \mathbb {E} [ (\mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {w}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma + \infty} + 1 3 \gamma \mathbb {E} [ \| \mathbf {W} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {w} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} _ {t} ^ {(2)} + \infty} ] \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $\mathbf{P}_t^{(2)}$  is entry-wise non-negative and  $\| r_t\|_{\infty}\leq \frac{9}{8}\gamma$  at the end. This gives the third result.

Lemma 4.22 ([BLSS20, Appendix A]). Let  $\mathbf{A} \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times n}$  be non-degenerate,  $\delta \in \mathbb{R}^m$  and  $p_i \geq \min\{1, k \cdot \sigma_i(\mathbf{A})\}$ . Further, let  $\widetilde{\delta} \in \mathbb{R}^m$  be chosen randomly where for all  $i \in [m]$  independently  $\widetilde{\delta}_i = \delta_i / p_i$  with probability  $p_i$  and  $\widetilde{\delta}_i = 0$  otherwise. Then

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \widetilde {\delta} ] = \delta \quad a n d \quad \mathbb {E} \| \widetilde {\delta} \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\mathbf {A})} ^ {2} \leq (1 + (1 / k)) \cdot \| \delta \| _ {\sigma (\mathbf {A})} ^ {2}.
$$

Lemma 4.23. for all non-degenerate  $\mathbf{A} \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times n}$ ,  $w, w' \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^m$  with  $w' \approx_{\gamma} w$  for  $\gamma > 0$ , and any non-negative vector  $v \in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^m$ , we have

$$
\left\| v \right\| _ {\mathbf {P} \left(\mathbf {W} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {A}\right) ^ {(2)}} \leq e ^ {4 \gamma} \left\| v \right\| _ {\mathbf {P} \left(\mathbf {W A}\right) ^ {(2)}}.
$$

Proof. Let  $\mathbf{D} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{W}'(\mathbf{W})^{-1}$ . Then, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} 0 \preceq \mathbf {P} \left(\mathbf {W} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {A}\right) = \mathbf {D W A} \left(\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {W} ^ {\prime 2} \mathbf {A}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {W D} \\ \preceq e ^ {2 \gamma} \mathbf {D W A} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {W} ^ {2} \mathbf {A}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {W D} \\ = e ^ {2 \gamma} \mathbf {D P} (\mathbf {W A}) \mathbf {D}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By Schur product theorem (that the entrywise product of two PSD matrices is PSD), we see that for any PSD  $\mathbf{M},\mathbf{N}\in \mathbb{R}^{m\times m}$  with  $\mathbf{M}\succeq \mathbf{N}$  it is the case that  $\mathbf{M}^{(2)} - \mathbf{N}^{(2)} = (\mathbf{M} - \mathbf{N})\circ (\mathbf{M} + \mathbf{N})$  is PSD (where we recall that  $\circ$  denotes entrywise product and use the shorthand  $\mathbf{M}^{(2)}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\mathbf{M}\circ \mathbf{M}$ ). Thus,  $\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{W}'\mathbf{A})\preceq e^{2\gamma}\mathbf{DP}(\mathbf{WA})\mathbf{D}$  implies

$$
\mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {A}) ^ {(2)} \preceq \left[ e ^ {2 \gamma} \mathbf {D P} (\mathbf {W A}) \mathbf {D} \right] \circ \left[ e ^ {2 \gamma} \mathbf {D P} (\mathbf {W A}) \mathbf {D} \right] = e ^ {4 \gamma} \mathbf {D} ^ {2} \mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W A}) ^ {(2)} \mathbf {D} ^ {2}.
$$

Hence, we have

$$
\left\| v \right\| _ {\mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {A}) ^ {(2)}} ^ {2} \leq e ^ {4 \gamma} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {D} ^ {2} v \right\| _ {\mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W} \mathbf {A}) ^ {(2)}} ^ {2} \leq e ^ {8 \gamma} \cdot \left\| v \right\| _ {\mathbf {P} (\mathbf {W} \mathbf {A}) ^ {(2)}} ^ {2}
$$

where we used that  $v$  and  $\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{WA})^{(2)}$  are coordinate-wise non-negative in the last step.

![](images/3fd442a37fbf86980573a4c7618cf7e4b09306a000fea1ebc243b72c6459a749.jpg)

Now, we can bound the change of  $\tau$  by bounding the change of  $w$ .

Lemma 4.24. In Algorithm 3 the vector  $\delta_{\tau} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \tau(x^{(\mathrm{new})}, s^{(\mathrm{new})}) - \tau(x, s)$  satisfies the following

$\| \mathbf{T}^{-1}\delta_{\tau}\|_{\infty}\leq 25\gamma$  
-  $\delta_{\tau} = \Lambda ((1 - 2\alpha)\mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_{x} - (1 + 2\alpha)\mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_{s}) + \eta$  with  $\| \mathbf{T}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\eta ]\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 2000\gamma^2$  , and  
$\| \mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{T}^{-1}\delta_{\tau})^{2}]\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 2000\gamma^{2}.$

In particular, we have  $\mathbb{E}\left[\| \mathbf{T}^{-1}\delta_{\tau}\|_{\phi^{\prime \prime}(v)}^{2}\right]\leq 500\gamma^{2}\| \phi^{\prime \prime}(v)\|_{\tau +\infty}^{*}$

Proof. Let  $\widetilde{w} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} x^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}s^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}$ ,  $\widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{new})} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} [x^{(\mathrm{new})}]^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}[s^{(\mathrm{new})}]^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}$ ,  $\widetilde{\mathbf{W}} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{Diag}(\widetilde{w})$ , and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_t \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{Diag}((1-t)w + tw^{(\mathrm{new})}))$  for all  $t \in [0,1]$ . Using  $\|\mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\infty} \leq 2\gamma$ ,  $\|\mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\infty} \leq 2\gamma$  (Lemma 4.19) and  $\alpha \in [0,\frac{1}{5}]$ , we have that  $w^{(\mathrm{new})} \approx_{5\gamma} w$ . Consequently, we can invoke Lemma 4.21 with  $w$ ,  $w^{(\mathrm{new})}$ ,  $\mathbf{W}_t$ , and  $\gamma$  set to be  $\widetilde{w}$ ,  $\widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{new})}$ ,  $\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_t$ , and  $5\gamma$  respectively. This gives the first result immediately and for the second and third result, for  $\delta_{\widetilde{w}} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{new})} - \widetilde{w}$  it suffices to bound

$$
K \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} 1 0 \| \mathbb {E} [ (\widetilde {\mathbf {W}} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\widetilde {w}}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma + \infty} + 6 5 \gamma \max _ {t \in [ 0, 1 ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\widetilde {w} ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}} [ \| \widetilde {\mathbf {W}} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {\widetilde {w}} | ] \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W}} _ {t} \mathbf {A}) + \infty} ].
$$

Now, for all  $t \in [0,1]$  let  $x_{t} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} x + t\delta_{x}$  and  $s_{t} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} x = t\delta s$ . We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {d}{d t} \ln \left(x _ {t} ^ {\frac {1}{2} - \alpha} s _ {t} ^ {- \frac {1}{2} - \alpha}\right) = \mathbf {X} _ {t} ^ {- \frac {1}{2} + \alpha} \mathbf {S} _ {t} ^ {\frac {1}{2} + \alpha} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{2} - \alpha\right) \mathbf {S} _ {t} ^ {- \frac {1}{2} - \alpha} \mathbf {X} _ {t} ^ {\frac {1}{2} - \alpha - 1} \delta_ {x} + \left(- \frac {1}{2} - \alpha\right) \mathbf {X} _ {t} ^ {\frac {1}{2} - \alpha} \mathbf {S} _ {t} ^ {- \frac {1}{2} - \alpha - 1} \delta_ {s} \right] \\ = \left(\frac {1}{2} - \alpha\right) \mathbf {X} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} - \left(\frac {1}{2} + \alpha\right) \mathbf {S} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \\ \end{array}
$$

and therefore we have

$$
\| \ln (\widetilde {w} ^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}) - \ln (\widetilde {w}) \| _ {\infty} \leq \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \left(\frac {1}{2} - \alpha\right) \mathbf {X} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} - \left(\frac {1}{2} + \alpha\right) \mathbf {S} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \right\| d t
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \left(\frac {1}{2} - \alpha\right) \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbf {X} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \right\| _ {\infty} d t + \left(\frac {1}{2} + \alpha\right) \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbf {S} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \right\| _ {\infty} d t \\ \leq \frac {1}{1 - (1 / 4 0)} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{2} - \alpha\right) \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \right\| _ {\infty} d t + \left(\frac {1}{2} + \alpha\right) \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \right\| _ {\infty} d t \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we used that  $\| \mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\infty}\leq \frac{1}{40}$  and  $\| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\infty}\leq \frac{1}{40}$ . Since  $\alpha \in [0,\frac{1}{5} ]$  this implies that  $\| \ln (\widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{new})}) - \ln (\widetilde{w})\|_{\infty}\leq 1 / 20$ , i.e.  $w^{(\mathrm{new})}\approx_{1 / 20}w$ . Consequently, Lemma 4.32 yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \widetilde {\mathbf {W}} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\widetilde {w}} \| _ {\infty} \leq \frac {1 + (1 / 2 0)}{1 - (1 / 4 0)} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{2} - \alpha\right) \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \right\| _ {\infty} d t + \left(\frac {1}{2} + \alpha\right) \int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \right\| _ {\infty} d t \right] \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \right\| _ {\infty} + \left\| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \right\| _ {\infty}. \tag {40} \\ \end{array}
$$

Consequently, for the first term in  $K$ , using  $\| \mathbb{E}[(\mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x)^2 ]\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 12\gamma^2$ ,  $\| (\mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s)^2\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 4\gamma^2$  (Lemma 4.19),  $\tau \geq \sigma$ , and (40) we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbb {E} [ (\widetilde {\mathbf {W}} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\widetilde {w}}) ^ {2} ] \| _ {\sigma + \infty} \leq 2 \| \mathbb {E} (\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x}) ^ {2} \| _ {\sigma + \infty} + 2 \| (\mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s}) ^ {2} \| _ {\sigma + \infty} \\ \leq 2 (1 2 \gamma^ {2}) + 2 (4 \gamma^ {2}) = 3 2 \gamma^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For the second term in  $K$ , we have again by (40) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} [ \| \widetilde {\mathbf {W}} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {\widetilde {w}} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\mathbf {W} _ {t} \mathbf {A}) + \infty} ] \leq \mathbb {E} [ \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {x} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W}} _ {t} \mathbf {A}) + \infty} + \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {s} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W}} _ {t} \mathbf {A}) + \infty} ] \\ \leq 1. 1 \mathbb {E} [ \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {x} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W A}}) + \infty} + \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {s} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W A}}) + \infty} ] \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used Lemma 4.23,  $\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}_t\approx_\gamma \widetilde{\mathbf{W}}$  and  $\gamma < 1 / 80$  at the end.

Since we sample coordinates  $i$  of  $\delta_{x}$  independently with probability at least  $10\sigma_{i}(\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}\mathbf{A})$ , Lemma 4.22 shows that

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {x} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W A}})} ] ^ {2} \leq \mathbb {E} [ \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {x} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W A}})} ^ {2} ] \leq 1. 2 \cdot \| \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {x} | ] \| _ {\sigma (\widetilde {\mathbf {W A}})} ^ {2}
$$

Using this, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} [ \| \widetilde {\mathbf {W}} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {\widetilde {w}} | \| _ {\mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\widetilde {\mathbf {W}} _ {t} \mathbf {A}) + \infty} ] \leq 1. 4 \| \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} | \delta_ {x} | ] \| _ {\sigma + \infty} + 1. 1 \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \| _ {\sigma + \infty} \\ \leq 2. 5 \gamma + 2. 5 \gamma = 5 \gamma \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\| \mathbf{X}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[|\delta_x|]\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 2\gamma$ $\| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 2\gamma$  (Lemma 4.18).

Hence, we have

$$
K \leq 1 0 (3 2 \gamma^ {2}) + 6 5 (5 \gamma) (2 4 \gamma^ {2}) \leq 2 0 0 0 \gamma^ {2}.
$$

# 4.3.3 Potential Decrease

Here we combine the analysis from Section 4.3.1 and Section 4.3.2 to analyze our short-step method for the LS weight function. We first provide two technical lemmas (Lemma 4.25 and Lemma 4.26 respectively) from [LS19] and [BLSS20] that we use in our analysis and then provide a sequence of results analogous to those we provided for the short-step method for the log barrier. In Lemma 4.27 we bound our first order expected progress from a step (analogous to Lemma 4.7), in Lemma 4.28 we bound the expected decrease in the potential (analogous to Lemma 4.10), and in Lemma 4.29 we give our main centrality improvement lemma (analogous to Lemma 4.11. Finally, in Lemma 4.30 we analyze the feasibility of a step (analogous to Lemma 4.12) and put everything together to prove that Algorithm 3 is a valid short step procedure in Lemma 4.31 (analogous to Lemma 4.5).

Lemma 4.25 (Lemma 47 in [LS19]). For any non-degenerate  $\mathbf{A} \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times n}$ , we have

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} (\mathbf {A}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\mathbf {A}) \| _ {\infty} \leq 1 a n d \| \boldsymbol {\Sigma} (\mathbf {A}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {P} ^ {(2)} (\mathbf {A}) \| _ {\tau (\mathbf {A})} \leq 1.
$$

Consequently,  $\| \mathbf{T}(\mathbf{A})^{-1}\mathbf{P}^{(2)}(\mathbf{A})\|_{\infty}\leq 1$  and  $\| \mathbf{T}(\mathbf{A})^{-1}\mathbf{P}^{(2)}(\mathbf{A})\|_{\tau (\mathbf{A})}\leq 1$

Lemma 4.26 (Lemma 26 in [BLSS20]). In Algorithm 3,  $\| \Psi \|_{\tau + \infty} \leq 1 - \alpha$  where  $\Psi \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} ((1 - 4\alpha^2)\mathbf{T}^{-1}\mathbf{\Lambda} - 2\alpha \mathbf{I})\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1/2}(\mathbf{I} - 2\mathbf{Q})\overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1/2}$ ,  $\mathbf{\Lambda} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{\Lambda}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})$  and  $\mathbf{Q} = \overline{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{H}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^\top$ .

Lemma 4.27 (First-order Expected Progress Bound). In Algorithm 3 we have

$$
\| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ \delta_ {x} ] + \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ \delta_ {s} ] - g - \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ \delta_ {\tau} ] \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq (1 - \alpha + 3 6 \epsilon / \alpha) \gamma .
$$

Proof. Let  $\mathbf{Q} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \overline{\mathbf{A}\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}$ . Note that

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} ] = (1 + 2 \alpha) (g - \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g) + \eta_ {x} - \mathbb {E} [ \delta_ {c} ]
$$

where  $\eta_{x} = \mathbb{E}[\mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_{x} - \overline{\mathbf{X}}^{-1}\delta_{x}]$ . Since  $\overline{\mathbf{X}}\approx_{\epsilon}\mathbf{X}$ , Lemma 4.32 and Lemma 4.24 show that

$$
\| \eta_ {x} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \| (\mathbf {I} - \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {X}) \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 2 \epsilon \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 4 \epsilon \gamma
$$

Further, Lemma 4.18 shows that  $\| \delta_c\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 11\epsilon \gamma /\alpha$

Similarly, we have

$$
\mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} = (1 - 2 \alpha) \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g + \eta_ {s}
$$

with  $\| \eta_s\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 4\epsilon \gamma$  . Lemma 4.24 shows that

$$
\mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\tau} = \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} ((1 - 2 \alpha) \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} - (1 + 2 \alpha) \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s}) + \eta_ {\tau}
$$

with  $\| \mathbb{E}\eta_{\tau}\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq 2000\gamma^{2}$ . Combining the three inequalities above, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\tau} - \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} - \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \right] \\ = \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} ((1 - 2 \alpha) \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} ] - (1 + 2 \alpha) \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s}) + \eta_ {\tau} - \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} ] - \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \\ = \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} ((1 - 2 \alpha) (1 + 2 \alpha) (g - \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g) - (1 + 2 \alpha) (1 - 2 \alpha) \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g) \\ - (1 + 2 \alpha) (g - \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g) - (1 - 2 \alpha) \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {Q} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g \\ + \eta_ {\tau} + (1 - 2 \alpha) \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \eta_ {x} - \eta_ {x} - (1 + 2 \alpha) \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \eta_ {s} - \eta_ {s} \\ = (\Psi - \mathbf {I}) g + \eta \\ \end{array}
$$

with

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Psi = ((1 - 4 \alpha^ {2}) {\bf T} ^ {- 1} {\pmb \Lambda} - 2 \alpha) \overline {{{\bf W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} ({\bf I} - 2 {\bf Q}) \overline {{{\bf W}}} ^ {1 / 2}, \\ \boldsymbol {\eta} = \eta_ {\tau} + (1 - 2 \alpha) \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \eta_ {x} - \eta_ {x} - (1 + 2 \alpha) \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Lambda} \eta_ {s} - \eta_ {s} + \mathbb {E} [ \delta_ {c} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

By Lemma 4.26, we have  $\| \Psi \|_{\tau + \infty} \leq 1 - \alpha$ . By the fact that  $\| \mathbf{T}^{-1}\mathbf{\Lambda} \|_{\tau + \infty} \leq 2$  (Lemma 4.25), we have that

$$
\| \eta \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 2 0 0 0 \gamma^ {2} + 2 4 \epsilon \gamma + 1 1 \epsilon \gamma / \alpha \leq 3 6 \epsilon \gamma / \alpha
$$

where we used  $\gamma \leq \frac{\epsilon}{500}$  and  $\alpha < 1/4$ . Hence, we have

$$
\| \mathbb {E} [ \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} - \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} - \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\tau} ] - g \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \| \Psi g \| _ {\tau + \infty} + \| \eta \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq (1 - \alpha + 3 6 \epsilon / \alpha) \gamma .
$$

We now have everything to bound the effect of a step in terms of  $\| \phi'(w) \|_{\tau + \infty}$  and  $\| \phi''(w) \|_{\tau + \infty}$ .

Lemma 4.28 (Expected Potential Decrease). In Algorithm 3 we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \Phi \left(x ^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}, s ^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}, \mu^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}\right) \right] \leq \Phi (x, s, \mu) + \phi^ {\prime} (v) ^ {\top} g + (1 - \alpha + 2 0 \epsilon / \alpha) \gamma \| \phi^ {\prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*} \\ + 6 0 0 0 \gamma^ {2} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof. We apply Lemma 4.34 with  $u^{(1)} = x$ ,  $\delta^{(1)} = \delta_x$ ,  $c^{(1)} = 1$ ,  $u^{(2)} = s$ ,  $\delta^{(2)} = \delta_s$ ,  $c^{(2)} = 1$ ,  $u^{(3)} = \mu \vec{1}$ ,  $\delta^{(3)} = \delta_{\mu} \vec{1}$ ,  $c^{(3)} = -1$ ,  $u^{(4)} = \tau(x, s)$ ,  $\delta^{(4)} = \delta_{\tau}$ ,  $c^{(4)} = -1$ . Note that,  $v$  in the context of Lemma 4.34 is precisely  $\frac{x s}{\mu \tau}$  and  $v^{(\mathrm{new})}$  in the context of Lemma 4.34 is precisely  $\frac{x^{(\mathrm{new})} s^{(\mathrm{new})}}{\mu^{(\mathrm{new})} \tau^{(\mathrm{new})}}$ .

Now, by Lemma 4.19, the definition of  $\gamma$ , and  $\epsilon \in [0,1/80]$  we have

$$
\| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} \| _ {\infty} \leq 2 \gamma \leq \frac {1}{5 0 \lambda} \leq \frac {1}{1 2 (1 + \| c \| _ {1} \lambda)}.
$$

Similarly, Lemma 4.19 implies

$$
\| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\infty} \leq 2 \gamma \leq \frac {1}{5 0 \lambda} \leq \frac {1}{1 2 (1 + \| c \| _ {1} \lambda)}.
$$

Further, by definition of  $r$  we have  $\mu^{-1}|\delta_{\mu}| \leq \gamma \leq (12(1 + \|c\|_1\lambda)^{-1})$ . Thus, Lemma 4.34 implies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \Phi (x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}}, s ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}}, \mu^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}}) \right] \\ \leq \Phi (x, s, \mu) + \phi^ {\prime} (v) ^ {\top} \mathbf {V} \left[ (\mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ \delta_ {x} ] + \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {s} - \mu^ {- 1} \delta_ {\mu} \vec {1} - \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} [ \delta_ {\tau} ]) \right] \\ + 1 0 \left(\mathbb {E} [ \| \mathbf {X} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\phi^ {\prime \prime} (v)} ^ {2} ] + \| \mathbf {S} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\phi^ {\prime \prime} (v)} ^ {2} + \| \mu^ {- 1} \delta_ {\mu} \vec {1} \| _ {\phi^ {\prime \prime} (v)} ^ {2} + \mathbb {E} \left[ \| \mathbf {T} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {\tau} \rbrack \| _ {\phi^ {\prime \prime} (v)} ^ {2} \right]\right). \tag {41} \\ \end{array}
$$

Now, since  $v \approx_{\epsilon} \vec{1}$ , the assumption on  $\delta_{\mu}$  implies

$$
| \mu^ {- 1} \delta_ {\mu} \phi^ {\prime} (v) ^ {\top} \mathbf {V} \vec {1} | \leq \mu^ {- 1} | \delta_ {\mu} | \exp (\epsilon) \| \phi^ {\prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*} \| \vec {1} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq 6 \epsilon \gamma \| \phi^ {\prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*}
$$

and

$$
\| \mu^ {- 1} \delta_ {\mu} \vec {1} \| _ {\phi^ {\prime \prime} (v)} ^ {2} = | \mu^ {- 1} \delta_ {\mu} | ^ {2} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {1} \leq (\epsilon \gamma / \sqrt {n}) ^ {2} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*} \| 1 \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \gamma^ {2} \| \phi^ {\prime \prime} (v) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {*}.
$$

Applying Lemmas 4.27, 4.19, and 4.24 yields the result.

Finally, we can prove the desired bound on  $\mathbb{E}[\Phi (x^{(\mathrm{new})},s^{(\mathrm{new})},\mu^{(\mathrm{new})})]$  by combining the preceding lemma with Lemma 4.36 and bounds on  $\| \cdot \|_{\tau +\infty}$ .

Lemma 4.29 (Centrality Improvement of LS Barrier Short-Step). In Algorithm 3 we have

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \Phi \left(x ^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}, s ^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}, \mu^ {\left(\text {n e w}\right)}\right) \right] \leq (1 - \lambda r) \Phi (x, s, \mu) + \exp (\lambda \epsilon / 6).
$$

Proof. Note that  $\| \overline{v} - w / \tau \|_{\infty} \leq \gamma$ . Consequently, we will apply Lemma 4.36 with  $\delta = \gamma$ ,  $c_1 = 1 - \alpha + 20\epsilon / \alpha$ ,  $c_2 = 6000$ , and  $U = \{x : \| x \|_{\tau + \infty} \leq 1\}$ . Using  $\gamma = \epsilon / (100\lambda)$ ,  $\lambda = 36\log(400m / \epsilon^2) / \epsilon$ , and  $\epsilon = \frac{\alpha^2}{240}$ , we have  $\delta \leq 1 / (5\lambda)$ ,  $\lambda\gamma \leq 1 / 8$ , and

$$
2 \lambda \delta + c _ {2} \lambda \gamma = 6 0 0 4 \lambda \gamma = 6 0. 0 4 \epsilon \leq 0. 2 6 \alpha^ {2} \leq \frac {\alpha^ {2}}{3} \leq \frac {1 - c _ {1}}{2}.
$$

Hence, the assumption in Lemma 4.36 is satisfied. Further, if  $\| x\|_{\infty}\leq \frac{\alpha}{16\sqrt{n}}$  then

$$
\| x \| _ {\tau + \infty} = C _ {\mathrm {n o r m}} \| x \| _ {\tau} + \| x \| _ {\infty} \leq \frac {\alpha}{1 6 \sqrt {n}} \left[ \frac {1 0}{\alpha} \cdot \sqrt {2 n} + 1 \right] \leq 1
$$

and consequently  $U$  contains the  $\| \cdot \|_{\tau +\infty}$  ball of radius  $\alpha /(16\sqrt{n})$ . Consequently, applying Lemma 4.36 and using that  $(1 - c_{1})\geq \alpha /2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} [ \Phi (x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}, s ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}, \mu^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}) ] \leq \Phi (x, s, \mu) - \frac {(1 - c _ {1}) \lambda \gamma u}{2} \Phi (x, s, \mu) + m \\ \leq \Phi (x, s, \mu) - \frac {\alpha^ {2} \lambda \gamma}{1 6 \sqrt {n}} \Phi (x, s, \mu) + m. \\ \end{array}
$$

The result follows from  $\exp \left(\frac{1}{6}\lambda \epsilon\right) \geq m$  and  $r = \frac{\epsilon\gamma}{\sqrt{n}}$ .

Next, we bound the infeasibility after a step (Lemma 4.30). This lemma is analogous to Lemma 4.12 and consequently the proof is abbreviated. (See Section 4.2.2 for further intuition.)

Lemma 4.30 (Feasibility Bound). In Algorithm 3 if  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\approx_{\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}$  then

$$
\left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {(\text {n e w})} - b \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X} ^ {(\text {n e w})} (\mathbf {S} ^ {(\text {n e w})}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A}} \leq 0. 5 \epsilon \gamma \left(\mu^ {(\text {n e w})}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \tag {42}
$$

and consequently, this holds with probability at least  $1 - r$ .

Proof. Recall that  $x^{\mathrm{(new)}} = x + \delta_x$  where  $\delta_x = \overline{\mathbf{X}}(g - \mathbf{R}\delta_r)$ ,  $\delta_r \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} (1 + 2\alpha)\delta_p + \delta_c$ , and

$$
\delta_ {p} = \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g \mathrm {a n d} \delta_ {c} = \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b).
$$

Consequently, we can rewrite the step as

$$
\delta_ {r} = \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1} d \text {w h e r e} d \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} (1 + 2 \alpha) \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {1 / 2} g + \mu^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b).
$$

Now, consider the idealized step  $x^{*} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} x + \delta_{x}^{*}$  where  $\delta_{x}^{*} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \overline{\mathbf{X}}(g - \delta_{r}^{*})$ , and  $\delta_{r}^{*} = \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{-1/2} \overline{\mathbf{A}}(\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top} \overline{\mathbf{A}})^{-1} d$ . Since,  $\mathbf{A}^{\top} \overline{\mathbf{X}} = \sqrt{\mu} \overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top} \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1/2}$  we have

$$
\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {*} = \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x + \sqrt {\mu} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {W} ^ {1 / 2} g - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1} d\right) = \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - (\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b) = b.
$$

Consequently,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b = \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} (x - x ^ {*}) = - \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {X}}} (\mathbf {R} \delta_ {r} - \delta_ {r} ^ {*}) = \sqrt {\mu} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \left(\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1}\right) d \\ = \sqrt {\mu} \left(\mathbf {I} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1}\right) d. \tag {43} \\ \end{array}
$$

Since  $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\approx_{\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}\approx_{\gamma}\overline{\mathbf{H}}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {I} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2} = \| (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \\ \leq \exp (\gamma) \| \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \leq 3 \gamma \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used Lemma 4.32 and  $(2\gamma + 4\gamma^2)\exp(\gamma) \leq 3\gamma$  for  $\gamma \leq 1/80$ . Consequently, combining with (25) yields that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {(\text {n e w})} - b \right\| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1}} = \sqrt {\mu} \left\| (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {I} - \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {R} \overline {{\mathbf {A H}}} ^ {- 1}) \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} d \right\| _ {2} \leq 3 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} \| d \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \\ \leq 3 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} \left((1 + 2 \alpha) \| \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} + \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Next, note that by design of  $g$  we have  $\| g\|_{\overline{\tau}}\leq (\gamma /C_{\mathrm{norm}})$  for  $C_\mathrm{norm} = 10 / \alpha$ . Further, we have  $\overline{\mathbf{W}}\approx_{4\epsilon}\overline{\tau}$  as  $\overline{w}\approx_{2\gamma}w$ ,  $w\approx_{\epsilon}\tau$ ,  $\tau \approx_{\gamma}\overline{\tau}$ , and  $\gamma \leq \epsilon$ . Since  $\epsilon \leq 1 / 80$  and  $\alpha \leq 1 / 4$  this implies

$$
\| \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \leq \exp (\gamma / 2) \| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top}} \leq \exp (\gamma / 2) \| \overline {{\mathbf {W}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} g \| _ {2}
$$

$$
\leq \exp (5 \epsilon / 2) \| g \| _ {\overline {{\tau}}} \leq \frac {\exp (5 \epsilon / 2) \alpha \gamma}{1 0} \leq \frac {2 \gamma}{1 + 2 \alpha}.
$$

Further, by the approximate primal feasibility of  $(x,s,\mu)$  we have

$$
\left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \right\| _ {\overline {{\mathbf {H}}} ^ {- 1}} \leq \exp (\gamma / 2) \left\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x - b \right\| _ {\mathbf {A} ^ {\top} \mathbf {X S} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {A}} \leq 2 \epsilon \gamma \sqrt {\mu}.
$$

Combining yields that

$$
\| \mathbf {A} ^ {\top} x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})} - b \| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {A}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {A}}}) ^ {- 1}} \leq 3 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} (2 \gamma + 2 \epsilon \gamma) \leq 9 \gamma \sqrt {\mu} \gamma \leq 0. 2 5 \epsilon \gamma \sqrt {\mu}.
$$

Now, since  $\| \mathbf{S}^{-1}\delta_s\|_{\infty}\leq 2\gamma$  and  $\| \mathbf{X}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\infty}\leq 2\gamma$  by Lemma 4.19 and  $\gamma \leq \epsilon /100$  we have that  $\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(\mathrm{new})}(\mathbf{S}^{(\mathrm{new})})^{-1}\mathbf{A}\approx_{\epsilon}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}$ . Combining with the facts that  $\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{A}\approx_{2\gamma}$ $\overline{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{A}}$ , and  $\mu^{(\mathrm{new})}\approx_{2\epsilon \gamma}\mu$  yields (42). The probability bound follows by the fact that  $q_{i}\geq C\sigma_{i}(\overline{\mathbf{A}})\log (n / (\epsilon r))\gamma^{-2}$  and Lemma 4 in [CLM  $^+$  15]. That is, the matrix  $\mathbf{R}$  corresponds to sampling each row of  $\mathbf{A}$  with probability at least proportional to its leverage score.

Next we prove the main result of this section, that Algorithm 3 is a valid SHORTSTEP procedure (see Definition 4.3).

Lemma 4.31 (LS Short Step). Algorithm 3 is a valid SHORTSTEP procedure (Definition 4.3) with weight function  $\tau(x, s) \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \sigma(\mathbf{X}^{1/2 - \alpha}\mathbf{S}^{-1/2 - \alpha}\mathbf{A}) + \frac{n}{m}\vec{1} \in \mathbb{R}^m$  for all  $x, s \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^m$  and parameters  $\lambda \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} 36\epsilon^{-1}\log(400m/\epsilon^2)$ ,  $\gamma \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \frac{\epsilon}{100\lambda}$ ,  $r \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \frac{\epsilon\gamma}{\sqrt{n}}$ ,  $\epsilon = \frac{\alpha^2}{240}$ , and  $\alpha = \frac{1}{4\log\left(\frac{4m}{n}\right)}$ , i.e.

1.  $\lambda \geq \frac{12\log(16m / r)}{\epsilon}$  
2.  $\mathbf{A}y^{(\mathrm{new})} + s^{(\mathrm{new})} = b$  for some  $y^{(\mathrm{new})}\in \mathbb{R}^n$  
3.  $\mathbb{E}[\Phi (x^{(\mathrm{new})},s^{(\mathrm{new})},\mu^{(\mathrm{new})})]\leq (1 - \lambda r)\Phi (x,s,\mu) + \exp (\lambda \epsilon /6)$  
4.  $\mathbb{P}\left[\| \mathbf{A}^\top x^{(\mathrm{new})} - b\|_{(\mathbf{A}^\top \mathbf{X}^{(\mathrm{new})}(\mathbf{S}^{(\mathrm{new})})^{-1}\mathbf{A})^{-1}}\leq \frac{\epsilon}{2}\sqrt{\mu^{(\mathrm{new})}}\right]\geq 1 - r.$

Proof. We provide the proofs of all the four parts as follows:

(1) Note that  $r = \frac{\epsilon\gamma}{\sqrt{n}} = \frac{\epsilon^2}{100\lambda\sqrt{n}} = \frac{\epsilon^3}{3600\log(400\beta)\sqrt{n}}$  for  $\beta = \frac{m}{\epsilon^2}$ . Thus,

$$
1 2 \log (1 6 m / r) \cdot \epsilon^ {- 1} \leq 1 2 \log (1 6 \beta^ {3 / 2} \cdot 9 6 0 0 \log (4 0 0 \beta)) \cdot \epsilon^ {- 1} \leq 1 2 \log ((4 0 0) ^ {3} \beta^ {2}) \cdot \epsilon^ {- 1} \leq \lambda
$$

where we used that  $\sqrt{x}\log x\leq x$  for  $x\geq 1$ $(400)^{3}\geq 16\cdot 9600\cdot 400$  , and  $\beta \geq 1$

(2) Note that  $\delta_s = \mathbf{A}\overline{\mathbf{H}}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{A}}^\top \overline{\mathbf{W}}^{1/2}g$ . Consequently,  $\delta_s \in \mathrm{im}(\mathbf{A})$  and this follows from the fact that  $\mathbf{A}y + s = b$  for some  $y \in \mathbb{R}^n$  by definition of  $\epsilon$ -centered.  
(3) This follows immediately from Lemma 4.29.  
(4) This follows immediately from Lemma 4.30.

# 4.4 Potential Function and Analysis Tools

Here we give general mathematical facts and properties of the potential function we used throughout this section in analyzing our IPM. First, we give a simple, standard technical lemma allowing us to relate different notions of multiplicative approximation Lemma 4.32. Then, in the remainder of this section we give facts about the potential function given in Definition 4.2.

Lemma 4.32. If  $\mathbf{M} \approx_{\epsilon} \mathbf{N}$  for symmetric  $PD$ $\mathbf{M}, \mathbf{N} \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times n}$  and  $\epsilon \in [0,1/2)$  then

$$
\| \mathbf {N} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {M} - \mathbf {N}) \mathbf {N} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \leq \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}.
$$

Consequently, if  $x \approx_{\epsilon} y$  for  $x, y \in \mathbb{R}_{>0}$  and  $\epsilon \in [0,1/2)$  then  $\|\mathbf{X}^{-1}y - \vec{1}\|_{\infty} \leq \epsilon + \epsilon^2$ .

Proof. These follow from the fact that for all  $\alpha \in \mathbb{R}$  with  $|\alpha| \leq 1/2$  we have  $1 + \alpha \leq \exp(\alpha) \leq 1 + \alpha + \alpha^2$  and therefore  $|\exp(\alpha) - 1| \leq |\alpha| + |\alpha|^2$ .

Lemma 4.33. For all  $w \in \mathbb{R}$  we have  $0 \leq \phi''(w) \leq \lambda \cdot (|\phi'(w)| + 2\lambda)$  and for all  $z_0, z_1$  with  $|z_1 - z_0| \leq \delta$  we have  $\phi''(z_1) \leq \exp(\lambda \delta) \phi''(z_0)$ .

Proof. Direct calculation shows that

$$
\phi^ {\prime} (w) = \lambda \cdot (\exp (\lambda (w - 1)) - \exp (\lambda (1 - w))) \mathrm {a n d} \phi^ {\prime \prime} (w) = \lambda^ {2} \cdot (\exp (\lambda (w - 1)) + \exp (\lambda (1 - w))).
$$

and therefore, clearly  $\phi''(w) \geq 0$  and the bounds on  $\phi''(w)$  follow from

$$
